It’s been obvious enough that I figured I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood when you're moving really slowly or quickly?\n\nPatient: Yeah. When I’m moving really slowly, it’s usually during the really low times. I’m barely eating or sleeping, and I feel guilty about pretty much everything. That’s also when I start hearing those headset beeps, like from work, and I get stuck thinking my old job’s system is still tracking me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

It’s been obvious enough that I figured I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood when you're moving really slowly or quickly?\n\nPatient: Yeah. When I’m moving really slowly, it’s usually during the really low times. I’m barely eating or sleeping, and I feel guilty about pretty much everything. That’s also when I start hearing those headset beeps, like from work, and I get stuck thinking my old job’s system is still tracking me.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned hearing headset beeps from work, are you actually hearing those sounds when there's no headset or device playing them?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It sounds real when it happens. I’ll hear the old headset beep, or sometimes a supervisor-type voice saying my name or “too slow,” even when my laptop’s closed and there’s no phone ringing or anything playing.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices you hear are actually your supervisor or someone else trying to communicate with you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it’s literally my supervisor talking to me, but it does feel connected to that job. Even during weeks when I’m doing okay, I get convinced the old ticketing software is somehow tracking me through job sites and marking me as unreliable. It’s hard to shake that feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that the old ticketing software is tracking you is true, even when you're told it's not possible?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when someone tells me it can’t work that way, part of me still believes it’s happening. Like I can have an okay week and still get convinced the old ticketing system is somehow following me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable. I know it probably sounds strange, but it feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like someone or something is intentionally trying to harm or harass you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I don’t know if I’d say “harm” exactly, but it feels like something is messing with me. Like that old ticketing software is still tracking me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable, even when I’m otherwise having an okay week. It feels intentional, like I’m being marked or harassed somehow.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe the old ticketing software is tracking you even when you're not experiencing those low times or hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Even when I’m not feeling especially low or hearing anything, I can still get stuck on the idea that the old ticketing software is following me through job sites and marking me as unreliable. I know it sounds unlikely, but it still feels true to me sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have periods where you don't believe the old ticketing software is tracking you?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it fades a bit, but I don’t think it fully goes away. Even during an okay week, I can still get convinced that the old ticketing software is tracking me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable. It just feels like it’s still there in the background.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices you hear and the feelings of being tracked are related to your mood, or do they seem to happen independently of how you're feeling?\n\nPatient: I think they can happen even when my mood isn’t that bad. I’ve had stretches of a few weeks where I was more or less normal, getting things done, and I still heard the supervisor voice or felt sure the old ticketing software was flagging me. So it doesn’t seem totally tied to being depressed.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling okay and not depressed, do you still believe the old ticketing software is tracking you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Even when I’m having a pretty okay week and don’t feel depressed, I can still get convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is tracking me through job sites and marking me as unreliable. It’s not as intense all the time, but the belief is still there.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling okay and not depressed, do you still think the old ticketing software's tracking is real?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I mean, I’ve had stretches for a few weeks where my mood was pretty close to normal and I was getting things done, but I still felt sure the old ticketing software was flagging me. Sometimes I’d still hear the supervisor voice too, even when I wasn’t really depressed.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It’s been obvious enough that I figured I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood when you're moving really slowly or quickly?\n\nPatient: Yeah. When I’m moving really slowly, it’s usually during the really low times. I’m barely eating or sleeping, and I feel guilty about pretty much everything. That’s also when I start hearing those headset beeps, like from work, and I get stuck thinking my old job’s system is still tracking me.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned hearing headset beeps from work, are you actually hearing those sounds when there's no headset or device playing them?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It sounds real when it happens. I’ll hear the old headset beep, or sometimes a supervisor-type voice saying my name or “too slow,” even when my laptop’s closed and there’s no phone ringing or anything playing.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices you hear are actually your supervisor or someone else trying to communicate with you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it’s literally my supervisor talking to me, but it does feel connected to that job. Even during weeks when I’m doing okay, I get convinced the old ticketing software is somehow tracking me through job sites and marking me as unreliable. It’s hard to shake that feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that the old ticketing software is tracking you is true, even when you're told it's not possible?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when someone tells me it can’t work that way, part of me still believes it’s happening. Like I can have an okay week and still get convinced the old ticketing system is somehow following me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable. I know it probably sounds strange, but it feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like someone or something is intentionally trying to harm or harass you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I don’t know if I’d say “harm” exactly, but it feels like something is messing with me. Like that old ticketing software is still tracking me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable, even when I’m otherwise having an okay week. It feels intentional, like I’m being marked or harassed somehow.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe the old ticketing software is tracking you even when you're not experiencing those low times or hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Even when I’m not feeling especially low or hearing anything, I can still get stuck on the idea that the old ticketing software is following me through job sites and marking me as unreliable. I know it sounds unlikely, but it still feels true to me sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have periods where you don't believe the old ticketing software is tracking you?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it fades a bit, but I don’t think it fully goes away. Even during an okay week, I can still get convinced that the old ticketing software is tracking me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable. It just feels like it’s still there in the background.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices you hear and the feelings of being tracked are related to your mood, or do they seem to happen independently of how you're feeling?\n\nPatient: I think they can happen even when my mood isn’t that bad. I’ve had stretches of a few weeks where I was more or less normal, getting things done, and I still heard the supervisor voice or felt sure the old ticketing software was flagging me. So it doesn’t seem totally tied to being depressed.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling okay and not depressed, do you still believe the old ticketing software is tracking you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Even when I’m having a pretty okay week and don’t feel depressed, I can still get convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is tracking me through job sites and marking me as unreliable. It’s not as intense all the time, but the belief is still there.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling okay and not depressed, do you still think the old ticketing software's tracking is real?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I mean, I’ve had stretches for a few weeks where my mood was pretty close to normal and I was getting things done, but I still felt sure the old ticketing software was flagging me. Sometimes I’d still hear the supervisor voice too, even when I wasn’t really depressed.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling okay and not depressed, do you still think the old ticketing software is actually tracking you?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Even when I’m having an okay week, I can still feel convinced it’s real — like the old ticketing software is somehow tracking me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable. It doesn’t always feel as intense, but I still believe it’s happening.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’ll hear the old headset beep, or sometimes a supervisor-type voice saying my name or “too slow,” even when my laptop’s closed and there’s no phone ringing or anything playing.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices you hear are actually your supervisor or someone else trying to communicate with you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it’s literally my supervisor talking to me, but it does feel connected to that job. Even during weeks when I’m doing okay, I get convinced the old ticketing software is somehow tracking me through job sites and marking me as unreliable. It’s hard to shake that feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that the old ticketing software is tracking you is true, even when you're told it's not possible?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when someone tells me it can’t work that way, part of me still believes it’s happening. Like I can have an okay week and still get convinced the old ticketing system is somehow following me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable. I know it probably sounds strange, but it feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like someone or something is intentionally trying to harm or harass you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I don’t know if I’d say “harm” exactly, but it feels like something is messing with me. Like that old ticketing software is still tracking me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable, even when I’m otherwise having an okay week. It feels intentional, like I’m being marked or harassed somehow.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe the old ticketing software is tracking you even when you're not experiencing those low times or hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Even when I’m not feeling especially low or hearing anything, I can still get stuck on the idea that the old ticketing software is following me through job sites and marking me as unreliable. I know it sounds unlikely, but it still feels true to me sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have periods where you don't believe the old ticketing software is tracking you?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it fades a bit, but I don’t think it fully goes away. Even during an okay week, I can still get convinced that the old ticketing software is tracking me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable. It just feels like it’s still there in the background.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices you hear and the feelings of being tracked are related to your mood, or do they seem to happen independently of how you're feeling?\n\nPatient: I think they can happen even when my mood isn’t that bad. I’ve had stretches of a few weeks where I was more or less normal, getting things done, and I still heard the supervisor voice or felt sure the old ticketing software was flagging me. So it doesn’t seem totally tied to being depressed.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling okay and not depressed, do you still believe the old ticketing software is tracking you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Even when I’m having a pretty okay week and don’t feel depressed, I can still get convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is tracking me through job sites and marking me as unreliable. It’s not as intense all the time, but the belief is still there.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling okay and not depressed, do you still think the old ticketing software's tracking is real?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It’s hard to shake that feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that the old ticketing software is tracking you is true, even when you're told it's not possible?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when someone tells me it can’t work that way, part of me still believes it’s happening. Like I can have an okay week and still get convinced the old ticketing system is somehow following me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable. I know it probably sounds strange, but it feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like someone or something is intentionally trying to harm or harass you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I don’t know if I’d say “harm” exactly, but it feels like something is messing with me. Like that old ticketing software is still tracking me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable, even when I’m otherwise having an okay week. It feels intentional, like I’m being marked or harassed somehow.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe the old ticketing software is tracking you even when you're not experiencing those low times or hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Even when I’m not feeling especially low or hearing anything, I can still get stuck on the idea that the old ticketing software is following me through job sites and marking me as unreliable. I know it sounds unlikely, but it still feels true to me sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have periods where you don't believe the old ticketing software is tracking you?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it fades a bit, but I don’t think it fully goes away. Even during an okay week, I can still get convinced that the old ticketing software is tracking me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable. It just feels like it’s still there in the background.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices you hear and the feelings of being tracked are related to your mood, or do they seem to happen independently of how you're feeling?\n\nPatient: I think they can happen even when my mood isn’t that bad. I’ve had stretches of a few weeks where I was more or less normal, getting things done, and I still heard the supervisor voice or felt sure the old ticketing software was flagging me. So it doesn’t seem totally tied to being depressed.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling okay and not depressed, do you still believe the old ticketing software is tracking you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Even when I’m having a pretty okay week and don’t feel depressed, I can still get convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is tracking me through job sites and marking me as unreliable. It’s not as intense all the time, but the belief is still there.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling okay and not depressed, do you still think the old ticketing software's tracking is real?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I mean, I’ve had stretches for a few weeks where my mood was pretty close to normal and I was getting things done, but I still felt sure the old ticketing software was flagging me. Sometimes I’d still hear the supervisor voice too, even when I wasn’t really depressed.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling okay and not depressed, do you still think the old ticketing software is actually tracking you?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Even when I’m having an okay week, I can still feel convinced it’s real — like the old ticketing software is somehow tracking me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable. It doesn’t always feel as intense, but I still believe it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you feel completely normal and the belief that the old ticketing software is tracking you goes away?\n\nPatient: Not really. I’ve had periods for a few weeks where my mood felt pretty normal and I was getting stuff done, but even then I’d still either hear that supervisor voice or feel sure the old ticketing software was flagging me somehow. It doesn’t fully go away.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling completely normal, do you still think the old ticketing software is actually tracking you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, even when I feel pretty normal, it doesn’t fully go away. I’ve had stretches for a few weeks where I was doing okay and getting things done, but I’d still hear that supervisor voice sometimes, or feel sure the old ticketing software was flagging me.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling completely normal, do you still think the voices you hear are real?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Even when my mood is closer to normal and I’m functioning okay for a few weeks, the voice still feels real to me. Like the supervisor voice is actually there, not just something I’m imagining.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling completely normal, do you still think the voices you hear are actually talking to you?\n\nPatient: Yes. Even when my mood is closer to normal for a few weeks and I’m getting things done, I still hear the supervisor voice sometimes. It still feels like it’s actually talking to me.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling completely normal, do you still think the old ticketing software is intentionally tracking you?\n\nPatient: Yes. Even during an okay week, when I’m not especially down or anything, I still get convinced the old ticketing software is somehow tracking me through job sites and marking me as unreliable. It feels intentional, like it’s flagging me on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Are the thoughts about the old ticketing software tracking you something that you think about for most of the day, every day?\n\nPatient: Not most of the day, every day, no. It comes up even when I’m having an okay week, and when it does I can get pretty convinced the old ticketing software is tracking me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable. But it’s not like I’m thinking about it nonstop all day.\n\nDoctor: Are the thoughts about the old ticketing software tracking you something that you think about for at least a few hours every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I’d say some days it can add up to a few hours, especially if I’m looking at job sites or thinking about work. Even when I’m having an okay week, I can still get convinced that the old ticketing software is tracking me and flagging me as unreliable.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that the old ticketing software is tracking you is something that could actually happen in real life?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know it probably sounds unlikely, but yeah, it feels like something that could happen. Like software can track things, and job sites have information, so in my mind it doesn’t feel impossible. That’s part of why I get convinced it’s flagging me as unreliable, even when I’m otherwise doing okay.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
